Tested: 6 checks incl. a 500-row random property (the reported drop always exits, one step less never does); 120 engine checks total.
- **The breakout volume marker (2026-09-14)** — the volume study's verdict surfaced as a FACT, never a rule (no advice rule reads volume; the study found the 200D upcross gets nothing from it). `computeStocks` stamps `volX` (today's volume / prior 20-day average) and `fresh3mHigh` (first close above the prior 60-session high — the study's E60 event, re-arming only after a slip back under); both empty until the first refresh after deploy, the advicePrev pattern. On a fresh-high day the Entry cell gains a small dot — amber at ≥1.5× (the confirmed kind whose sign survived the 2020 split), faint below (the quiet kind with no measured edge) — with the study's one sentence as the tooltip; the Entry family panel carries the same line labeled "Context — not a rule input", and the hover card a Breakout row. Help §12 documents both.
- `whipsaw.neutral_band_pct` is implemented (a band around the 200D counts as neither side); `persistence_days` is deliberately absent rather than a dead knob — it needs day-over-day state.
- **The chatbot reads the Advice columns now (2026-09-12), with attribution required** — the bot may report "the Balanced rules read this as Buy: <flag>" but never present it as its own recommendation; the open decision is closed. **Momentum and Overall went the other way the same day**: the owner pulled every momentum-family field from `CHAT_FIELDS` ("momentum is not working the way I thought"), and Overall went with it because it is 65% momentum — Quality stays as the one momentum-free composite. The rules' does-not-contain inventory names the withholding explicitly and forbids improvising momentum verdicts from the raw returns the bot still has. Advice under Balanced never reads the momentum composite (`use_momentum` off), so nothing is smuggled back. **The bot also gets a `trendTimeline` per row** — the trend state's last ~12 months as dated runs ("2026-01-07 Downtrend → 2026-01-20 Breakdown → …"), computed by `Action.trendTimeline()` during the refresh from the same 650-day archive window `pastMomentum()` already reads (zero extra queries), stamped into the snapshot. Bar-derived only, so it is exact where a full advice history would be fiction — **with one dated boundary**: from 2026-08-30 (fundamentals recording) and fully from 2026-09-13 (earnings-date columns), a full advice REPLAY is a pure function of stored base data, recomputable under current rules on demand. The owner chose replay over storage ("why should I store it"); nothing before 2026-08-30 is recoverable by any means.

## News
Per-stock headlines — **headline / source / url / timestamp only, never bodies** — from a swappable provider in `news.js` (the momentum.js shape: parsing and selection pure, orchestration in server.js). Default provider is **Google News RSS, keyless**, queried by company *name* ("<name>" stock) because Google is not ticker-tagged — expect occasional neighbour-stock noise in a feed; `FINNHUB_API_KEY` switches to Finnhub's ticker-tagged `/company-news` (free tier: 60 calls/min, personal use), and `NEWS_PROVIDER=off` kills the feature (routes serve empty, pages show quiet states).

- **Storage**: `news` (id = url-hash, per-symbol, pruned to 21 days / 25 items) + `news_state` (per-symbol fetch clock, separate so an empty feed still counts as fetched). Both in `SYMBOL_TABLES`, so a dropped ticker takes its headlines along. Writes are one wholesale batch per symbol.
- **News is its own button and rides NOTHING (2026-09-18, owner's instruction: "don't mix the news refresh with any API data refresh").** `POST /api/news/refresh` does ONE batch of the stalest stocks and says how many remain; the page loops it, the same shape the price refreshes use, because a serverless function must not be asked to hold hundreds of network fetches in one request. The button is on `/news-runs`, and the console's **Refresh news** row deep-links `?do=refresh` — the `/refreshes?do=` pattern. `topUpNews()` is deleted.
  - **Why it was moved.** It was awaited inside the refresh (nothing may run after a response on this platform), which put a dozen network fetches inside the request that also priced the universe and wrote the snapshot. On 2026-09-18 a refresh recorded its round at 78s, wrote a correct snapshot at 10:10, and was then cut before `finishRun` — logged **`abandoned`, with no error**, while the data was perfectly fine. The owner reported it as "price refresh failed again"; it had not failed. A refresh is one request and the tail was most of it.
  - **Staleness is the CLOCK, and the same test decides both what is picked and what `remaining` counts.** The first cut let "holds no headlines" make a stock stale, which is fine as an ORDERING boost (an empty feed is retried ahead of the rotation) and fatal as a termination test: a stock the provider has nothing on would be re-picked forever while the run reported itself done. Caught by a test that looped to completion and then asked for one more batch.
  - Batches log `trigger: 'manual'` with no `refresh_run_id`, so `/news-runs` no longer shows them as riding a refresh round. The stock page's own stored-or-fetch on a six-hour TTL is unchanged — that one is user-driven and costs nothing.
- *Superseded:* **Coverage without a schedule**: `topUpNews()` rides every refresh (each cron round included), topping up the `NEWS_TOPUP_PER_REFRESH` (12) stalest symbols, never-fetched first — the nightly job's ~20 rounds cycle the whole universe in one night. Started early in the round and **awaited before the response** — it was fire-and-forget until 2026-09-15, and on Vercel a function is frozen the moment it responds: batches were suspended mid-fetch, then every 6-second abort timer fired on the next wake, so two batches logged 54s and 279s with all 24 fetches "aborted due to timeout" for refreshes that took 19s. **Nothing may run after a response on this platform.** The batch still never rejects, so a failed news write never fails a refresh (the bars rule). `GET /api/news?symbol=` is stored-or-fetch with a 6-hour TTL so a visited stock page stays fresh regardless; on a provider failure it serves the stored set, stale beating nothing.
- **Frontends (deliberately only two)**: a News card on `/stock/<SYMBOL>` between the chart and the fields (ten headlines, escaped, http(s)-vetted links out, `target=_blank rel=noopener`), and one `News` row in the hover card's Info group — injected onto the rows by `loadLatestNews()` after the table paints (`GET /api/news/latest`, stored-only, one query — the sparklines rule), never a table column. The analysis page shows nothing because its rows never get `newsLatest`; `CHAT_FIELDS` still excludes news.
- Verified: 18 checks in the news suite (RSS entities/CDATA, publisher-suffix strip, dedupe caps, stalest picker, renderer escaping incl. a `javascript:` url refused), plus a live boot fetching real MU headlines through the whole path.

### The News column (screener)
**A News column in the Info group, and a panel behind it (2026-09-16).** Until now the only per-stock way in was hovering a row or opening its page; this makes the whole thing one click from the table.

- **The cell is the age of the latest stored headline** (`2h`, `4d`) with a **count badge** when the stock has more than one from the last week, brightened when the latest is under a day old. **Blank means two different things and the cell separates them**: an em-dash is "checked, nothing stored" with the tooltip saying when it was last checked, a faint dot is "not looked at yet" — headlines are topped up a dozen stocks per refresh round, so the second case is common and must not read as "no news". The latest headline is the cell's tooltip.
- **Sorting is on the timestamp, not the age**, because a new column starts descending and descending has to mean freshest first. Stocks with nothing stored sort last.
- **`GET /api/news/latest` now returns `{items, counts, checked}`** — the latest per symbol as before, a 7-day count per symbol (`newsCountsSince`, one bounded group-by over the pruned table: 5,849 rows on 2026-09-16, allowlisted in the query-plan test with that number) and `news_state`'s fetch clock. All three are guest-filtered. The screener already loaded this after the table painted for the hover card, so the column costs one extra small query, not a new request.
- **Clicking a cell opens `#newsPop`**: up to ten headlines with source and age, each opening the publisher in a new tab, plus a link to the stock page. It reads `GET /api/news?symbol=`, which is stored-or-fetch on a six-hour rule — so **a stock nobody has looked at gets its headlines on the first click**, at no API cost (the provider is free and keyless). Escape, an outside click or a resize closes it.
- **`?force=1` checks now** rather than waiting out the six-hour rule — the stock page's **Check now** button, beside a line saying when that stock was last checked. The provider is free and keyless, so a forced check costs no API credits; the server keeps a **one-minute floor per symbol** so a held-down button cannot hammer anyone, and a refused force answers normally with `forced: false` rather than an error. The route now returns `checkedAt` so the page can say "checked just now". Verified with the provider stubbed: 12 checks (no call inside the TTL, a call when forced, the headlines stored, the clock moving, the second force inside a minute refused, the button's states).
- Verified in a browser over four seeded stocks (fresh / old / checked-but-empty / never-checked): 15 checks — the endpoint's three parts, each cell's shape and tooltip, the panel's list with sources and links, the stock-page link, Escape, the "nothing stored" wording, and the sort order.

### The news ticker (screener)
**A slow crawl of headlines published in the last 12 hours, under the table (2026-09-15, owner's spec: slow, hideable, 12h then stale).** **It follows the table** — the owner first chose the whole universe, then changed it the same day: the strip shows only stocks `visibleStocks()` leaves on screen (portfolio, sector, industry, advice, Changed, column filters), the label reads "last 12h · stocks in view" while narrowed, and a view with no recent news says so in the strip rather than hiding it (hiding would resize the table on every filter change; the strip hides only when nothing in the universe is recent). **`render()`'s empty-table branch returns early, so it calls `paintTicker()` itself** — missed on the first cut and caught by the test. `GET /api/news/recent` reads stored rows only (`readRecentNews`, published_at ≥ now − 12h — ISO strings, so the comparison is lexical), keeps at most 2 per stock and one row per URL, universe-filtered and guest-filtered, and sends **every stock's share** (147 items / 70 KB at the time) — a universe-wide top 60 would leave most filters empty; the page takes the first 60 of what is in view. Item: `SYM ▲2.3% · headline · source · 2h`; the symbol opens the stock page, the headline the article (http/https only), both in a new tab.

- **`#ticker` is `flex: none` inside the page column**, so showing it takes ~44px from the table rather than growing the page; every visibility change calls `paintWindow(true)` because the windowed table sizes itself from `.table-wrap`'s height and no resize event fires.
- **Two copies of the items crawl by exactly one copy's width** (`translateX(-50%)`), so the loop has no seam; `--tk-dur` is set from the measured width at **60px/s**. Hover, keyboard focus and the pause button stop it (`animation-play-state`) — pausing moving content is a WCAG requirement, not a nicety. **Reduced motion gets no crawl**: one headline at a time, swapped every 6s with an opacity fade.
- **Rebuilt only when its items or the prices change** (`tickerKey` = urls + `lastUpdated`): `paintTicker()` runs at the end of every `render()`, and rebuilding the track restarts the crawl, so a sort or a filter must not. Re-read every 10 minutes, and the 12-hour window is enforced client-side too, so an item ages out between reads.
- **On by default; hiding is stored** (`prefs.tickerOff`, boolean-only in the PUT whitelist). The × on the strip hides it; `News ticker` at the top of the ⋯ menu toggles it back. Hidden when the window holds nothing.
- **Fixed first, because a ticker would have advertised it: news was searched by the quoted LEGAL name**, which matches mostly old filings that fall outside the 21-day window — 36 of 271 stocks held no headlines. `newsNamesFor()` now searches the display name (override, else `deriveShortName`), measured on all 36: legal-name queries found anything for 28 and stored nothing that survived the window; short names found 22-25 results for all 36, and at least as many for the control group. A stock holding no headlines whose fetch is past the TTL is now picked ahead of the rotation in `topUpNews`. **The short-name rule gained whole `American Depositary Shares` / `Sponsored ADR` / `N.V.` / `S.A.` tails** — 8 of 271 display names changed, every one a fix (AstraZeneca PLC American → AstraZeneca, Toyota Motor Corporation Sponsored → Toyota Motor, Adyen N.V → Adyen, NXP, STMicroelectronics, Spotify Technology, Ericsson, Nano Dimension); 14 rule cases pass.
- **Cadence caveat**: headlines are only as fresh as their last fetch — 12 stocks per refresh round plus stock-page visits. A 12-hour window therefore shows mostly the stocks fetched recently; the News runs page's coverage tile says how many that is.
- Verified: the filtered strip headlessly (11 checks — a sector shows only its stocks and all of them, a sort keeps the crawl, an empty table and a newsless view keep the strip with its message, one stock shows only its headlines, clearing returns to everything); the endpoint on live data (≤2 each, none older than 12h, no duplicate URLs; a guest saw only preview symbols); TM and AZN fetched through the fixed search (25 new headlines each, from nothing); a headless run on the real page over those headlines — 15 checks normal, 10 under `--force-prefers-reduced-motion`.

### The news log (`/news-runs`)
**Every batch of headline fetches is logged (2026-09-15)** — `news_runs` (one per batch) and `news_run_items` (one per stock in it), read by `/news-runs` (admin, a `News runs` row in the console's Data section). Two triggers: `refresh` (a refresh round's top-up, linked to the refresh run it rode on via `refresh_run_id`, whose kind the page shows — "Nightly round", "Fill missing round") and `page` (a stock page fetching headlines older than `NEWS_TTL_MS`).

- **`runNewsBatch(picks, meta)`** is the one path both triggers take: it logs each stock's outcome as it lands (fetched, **new** — not already held — and stored after the write) and closes the batch `complete`, `partial` or `failed`. **`writeNews()` now returns `{added, stored}`**, reading the symbol's prior ids and its final count in the same batch as the write, so the log costs no extra round trip.
- **`topUpNews` is called after `finishLiveRefresh` reads the run state**, so the batch knows its refresh run; it was the first line of the function before.
- **`abandoned`** = still `running` three minutes after starting (`NEWS_RUN_ABANDON_MS`). The top-up is fire-and-forget, and on serverless an instance can be frozen once the response has gone — this is the status that would show it happening. Swept lazily by every read.
- **The page**: coverage (last fetch under a day / under a week / older / never, the stalest stock, rounds for a full pass at 12 a round, provider), headlines stored (total, stocks with at least one, **stocks fetched but holding nothing**), the last 7 days (batches, fetches, failure rate, new headlines, batches that never finished), fetches per day for 14 days with new headlines as a line, filterable history, and a per-stock dialog linking to the batch's refresh run (`/refreshes?run=<id>` opens that run).
- **Retention**: 30 days of batches, 14 of per-stock rows, pruned after each batch.
- **First real reading, 2026-09-15: 36 of 271 stocks have been fetched and hold no headlines** (MS, RBRK, SKHY, BABA, CMCSA among them). The Google query is the quoted legal name + "stock", and a quoted "SK hynix Inc. American Depositary Receipt" matches nothing — the short name is the likely fix.
- Verified end to end locally: a stale stock page (EBAY, 13h) logged one batch — 25 fetched, 4 new, 25 stored, 668ms — and a second open inside the TTL logged nothing; route walk (anon/guest 302 and 403, admin 200, unknown batch 404); headless page over synthetic history.

## Logos — built and removed the same day
**There are no company logos anywhere (owner's call, 2026-09-14): the provider's marks aged badly and sat on white boxes, and the whole pipeline came out the day it went in** — the `logos` table (dropped from Turso), the fetch/override/serving routes, the admin button, its receipt email, the LogoFX transparency module, and every `<img>`. Tickers render as **typographic monograms** (`.lg` on the promo cards) or plain text; the stock masthead is back to the symbol alone. What the episode measured, kept for the next time logos come up: Twelve Data `/logo` costs 1 credit/symbol and its image URL is keyless (~7KB 128px JPEG) but the marks lag rebrands by years; Wikimedia thumbnails moved to `thumb.wikimedia.org`, exist only at pre-rendered sizes, and require a User-Agent; ~11 of 93 symbols (ETFs mostly) had no mark at all. If logos ever return, the per-symbol URL override was the workable shape and the bulk provider was the problem.

## The basket page
**`/portfolio/<name>` — one portfolio aggregated, for shared portfolios and a member's own lists (`my:<Name>` in the URL), reached from a chart-arrow beside every row in the portfolio picker (2026-09-14).** A screener view, deliberately not a tracker: no positions exist anywhere in this product, and the owner ruled out P&L tracking explicitly. First cut is three cards; more modules (composition, movers, earnings, news) are the planned second pass.

- **The curve** (`GET /api/basket?name=&days=`, requireMember): equal dollars at the window start, held — `mean(close/start)` per session over the union-of-dates axis, symbols with no history at the window start sit out and are counted in the caption. **The benchmark is the whole universe on the same convention**, not SPY: the benchmark is deliberately never archived, and "did my list beat my own screener" is the better question anyway. One `readBarsFor(all, since)` covers both curves (basket ⊆ universe); measured 1.27s at 1Y × 93 symbols, cached per range client-side. Ranges 1M/3M/6M/1Y; the chart is a hand-rolled dual-line SVG (no `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, so SVG text labels are safe here).
- **Stance**: trend-state stacked bar + counts, % above the 200D, Balanced advice rollup, and today's advice changes within the list.
- **Returns next to the whole screen**: mean of each stock's own horizon return (1W→1Y), list vs all vs difference — an equal-weight reading, the caption says so. Verified live: Mag 7 +11.0% 1Y against the universe's +60.4%, both curves anchored at exactly 1.0.
- Personal lists resolve against the CALLER's own `user_portfolios` via `prefsKey`, so one member can never read another's; guests are refused by requireMember and the page redirects them.

## Cards, and the module behind them
**`private/cards.js` owns every card: its markup, its wording AND its styles** (2026-09-14). Two surfaces draw from it — `/promo` (owner) and `/cards` (members) — and a second copy of "Top gainers this week" would have drifted inside a week, the same reason `rowcard.js`, `screens.js` and `action.js` exist. The contract is one call: `Cards.build(id, ctx)` with `{stocks, myLists, size, opts, getBasket}` returning the card's inner HTML. **The module reads no DOM and issues no requests** — the host hands it control values by control id and a `getBasket(days)` that returns the archive payload or null while it loads, which is what makes all sixteen card variants testable off-page in a `vm`. Styles ride along in a template literal injected by `Cards.injectStyle()`, so a grammar added to one page cannot be styled only in the other.

**A card is dated by its DATA, not by the reader's clock (2026-09-16).** `dateStr()` stamped `new Date()`, so a card built on a Saturday — or after a refresh had failed — printed today's date over Thursday's prices, and that card goes on Instagram. It is the freshest `latestDate` across the rows now, derived in `build()`, parsed at **local noon** because `new Date('2026-09-16')` is UTC midnight and renders as the 15th west of Greenwich.

**The card prints the refresh TIME beside that date** (owner: "why can't you show the same on the visual itself") — `Sep 16, 2026 · 4:37 PM ET` in the header pill, from `ctx.updatedAt`, which the studio, `/cards` and the phone's post endpoint all now pass. **Always America/New_York, never the builder's own zone**: the card is a baked image, so whoever renders it would otherwise bake their clock into something other people read, and the market it describes keeps one timezone. A host that does not supply the time gets the date alone rather than an invented one.

**Both studio pages say when the prices were pulled** (`#freshLine`, owner's request — "this will give me confidence that it is latest data"): the refresh clock, how long ago, and the day the cards will carry, from the `updatedAt` that `/api/stocks` already returned and both pages were discarding. It goes **amber past 26 hours**, because the one thing worth noticing before posting is that the numbers are yesterday's. The phone's post sheet carries the same fact as "data as of …". Verified on fresh and week-old snapshots: the wording, the flag, and the card stamping Sep 9 rather than today.

**Cards label by NAME, not by ticker** (2026-09-14, owner's instruction). `nameOf(row)` is `shortName || name || symbol` and `symOf(sym)` looks a bare ticker up through it; every card's rows, tiles, tracks, rankings, verdict roll-calls, scatter labels and the Intro live-data slides run through them. A feed reads a company faster than a four-letter code, and the screener carries a Short name column now, so the string exists on every row. The columns were restyled with it — the mono face gives way to the sans one, widths roughly double, and every one truncates with an ellipsis rather than wrapping a post into two lines.

**The Movers time window has calendar windows beside the rolling ones (2026-09-15)**: *This week* (`wtd`) runs from the last close before the current week's Monday, *This month* (`mtd`) from the last close before the 1st — "current" being the week and month of the freshest bar, so a Saturday post shows the week just ended. *Week* and *Month* stay **rolling** (5 and 21 sessions — the last 7 and ~30 days) and are labelled so in the dropdown; the card text for Week changed from "this week" to "past week", since that phrase now belongs to week-to-date. **The snapshot carries no calendar anchors**, so `GET /api/period-anchors` (member, cached 10 minutes) reads each symbol's last close before each boundary (`closesBefore`, ~80ms a boundary) and the studio stamps `wtdPct` / `mtdPct` = price ÷ anchor on its rows; a row without an anchor is simply not ranked. **An anchor more than 7 days older than its boundary is dropped** — a symbol with a gap in its bars (AVB, no bars after 2026-08-24, at the time) would otherwise show a move spanning weeks under a "this week" label. Only `/promo` offers the two windows; `cards.js` supports them wherever a host stamps the fields. Verified: the endpoint against an independent query (NVDA −2.25% week, −3.36% month), and headlessly on real prices (10 checks — both windows rank their own leaders, Week still ranks the rolling 7 days, compare column, side by side).

**The Movers card can carry the verdict** (2026-09-14, `movAdv`): off by default, else a rule set from `ADV_PROFILES`, scored in the browser through `advScored()` — the same cached engine pass the Advice cards use, so it costs nothing beyond the first profile. **The word rides UNDER the name, not in a column of its own**: "Sell Immediately" would need ~170px, and with the comparison column on there is not that much left; stacked it costs no width and about 2px of row height. **The rule set is named in the KICKER, not in a paragraph** — the first cut put it in an `s-sub` and the five extra lines of prose were most of what pushed the card over its own masthead; the attribution still has to be there, so it moved somewhere free. A row the engine cannot score simply has no second line. **The side-by-side layout is excluded** — it already runs two rankings in half the width apiece — and the promo control hides itself when Direction is "Both, side by side". `/cards` passes no `movAdv`, so the member card is unchanged; exposing it there is one option element.

**A card is a fixed artboard and cannot scroll, so a list too tall does not clip — it sits on top of the masthead**, because `.s-body` centres its content. The ranked list therefore picks its rhythm from the row count, the artboard and whether a verdict line is on (`ROOM` / `RHYTHM` in `tplMovers`): default, then `tight`, then `tighter`, each shrinking the bar, the name and the number **together**, since a short bar beside a full-size number reads as a mistake rather than as a denser card. Past the tightest rhythm the list is **trimmed** rather than overflowed — the same per-shape cap the side-by-side card takes — and because the rows are sorted by the move it is always the smallest that go, with the title saying "Top gainers" rather than a count so nothing on the card becomes untrue. Square at Top 15 with a comparison column lands there (11 rows); it overflowed before the verdict feature existed too. **Verified by a harness that measures the artboard independently of the builder's own estimates** — 72 combinations of shape × count × advice × compare × direction, 0 overflowing, tightest fit 46px of slack.

**Side by side on a Story fills the frame (2026-09-15)** — it used to render the post layout, dense past ten rows (names cut at 92px), bunched at the top of a 1920px artboard. On `sz-story` each row is two lines (name and move above, the bar full width beneath), the rows sit in `.mlist` and spread with `space-evenly`, and **the shorter column is padded with invisible `.mpad` rows** so the nth gainer stays level with the nth loser. Six rows or fewer get `.few` (40px type, 30px bars), because five rows spread through a story read as lines floating in a frame. Post and square are unchanged. Verified by measuring the real page, 3 shapes × 3 scopes × Top 5/10/15 × two windows: no overflow anywhere, columns level everywhere, the list uses 94-98% of the story height, at most 2 names in 30 truncated on a story.

**Two places deliberately keep the ticker.** The **Chart** card: its legend chips and end tags sit in inches of space beside a drawn line, where only a short code fits — the owner excluded it by name. And the Intro **mock** slide, which draws a fake screener row beside its star: that column really does show the symbol, so naming it would make the illustration lie about the product. The fundamentals scatter compromises instead — it names its eight outlier dots, anchors a label back towards the middle when its dot sits in the right half, and clips past 22 characters, because a name is many times wider than a ticker and would otherwise run off the artboard.

**`/cards` is the member room** (signed-in, not guest; a row at the top of the Research picker): the three data cards — Movers, Chart, Advice — one shape (the 4:5 post), controls in a bar above rather than a studio panel, and **Save image**, the same data-URI exporter. Keeping the export is deliberate: every card carries the wordmark, the domain and the disclaimer, so a member sharing one is distributing the brand with its rules attached. Personal lists appear in every scope picker, resolved against the caller's own rows. **`/promo` stays the owner's superset** — it keeps the other two artboard sizes, the crop guide, and the two templates that are the brand's own voice (Intro carousels, Announcement). Guests get neither page.

## The public side: a landing page and a blog
**Three pages a stranger can read (2026-09-16, owner's request — "a home page like tastytrade.com, and a blog open to all visitors").** Until now the whole site was a door: anonymous `/` redirected to a password form that never said what the site was.

- **`/` serves `private/landing.html` when signed out**, the screener when signed in — the one-line change in the root route. Structure follows the reference site (nav → hero with two CTAs → stat strip → what it is → an illustrated table row → the research-honesty section → data/freshness → FAQ → closing CTA → footer with the not-advice disclaimer), in this app's own voice and palette. **The illustrated row is labelled as an illustration** with made-up names; the two real numbers come from `GET /api/public-stats` (universe count + snapshot timestamp, cached 10 min, deliberately cheap — no row counts).
- **`/blog` and `/blog/:slug` are PUBLIC and RENDERED ON THE SERVER.** The pages are templates in `private/` with `%PLACEHOLDERS%` filled by the route, so a post's words are in the HTML: a blog that needs JavaScript to show its text is one that search engines and bad connections cannot read. Each post page carries its own `<title>`, description, `og:` tags and canonical link. `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml` list the published posts.
- **Posts live in `posts` (slug primary key, `status` draft|published)** and are written at **`/posts`** (admin) — title, address, summary, markdown body, Save draft / Publish / Preview / Delete. Publishing needs no deploy. `published_at` is stamped on the FIRST publish and never moves on an edit, so the index does not reshuffle when a typo is fixed; a rename carries the post to the new slug rather than leaving two. The editor's preview renders through the SERVER's markdown, because a second renderer in the page would drift from what readers see.
- **`renderMarkdown()` escapes first, then formats** — the chat renderer's rule, and it matters more here: `<script>` in a draft comes out as text, and a `javascript:` link keeps its words and loses its href. Supported: headings (`#` becomes an `h2`, since the page owns the `h1`), lists, quotes, fenced code, bold/italic/inline code, `---`, and http(s) or internal links.
- **Drafts are invisible to everyone but the owner** — enforced in the route (`publishedOnly`), not in the page: `/blog/<draft>` is a 404 and the public list and sitemap omit it.
- The login page gained **Read the blog** and **What is Tickr Lab?** links, and the landing page's Try it button uses `/login?guest=1`, which the login page acts on — the guest door stays in one place rather than being POSTed from a public page.
- **The admin API routes had to move below `requireAdmin`'s definition** — registered beside the public ones they threw `Cannot access 'requireAdmin' before initialization` at boot, exactly as the `/api/db-stats` note warns. `node --check` passes that; only running it catches it.
- Verified: 34 checks against the real server with a password set, so the public pages are tested as a stranger sees them (publish/draft/rename/delete, the publish date surviving an edit and a rename, the escaping and link rules, anonymous access to `/`, `/blog`, a post, the sitemap and robots, 404s for a draft and an unknown slug, 403s for the admin API, the editor's redirect) plus 20 browser checks at 1440px and 400px (no sideways scroll on any page, headings present, the live stock count, no console errors).

## The phone page
**`/m` — a separate page for browsing screens on a phone, signed-in including guests (2026-09-16).** The screener is a 80-column table on a page that deliberately does not scroll, and it was never going to become responsive; the owner's call was a second page rather than a rewrite. **It has no settings of its own** — the only control is which *view* a row shows, and the views themselves are configured on a desktop at **`/mobile-setup`** (admin, a Mobile setup row in the console's Screener section).

- **The server runs the screen, not the phone.** `/api/stocks` is ~1.3 MB; trimmed to what a row card can render it is still 569 KB, which is not a payload for a phone on mobile data. So `GET /api/m/screen?id=&view=` filters, sorts and **formats** server-side and returns ~20 rows — **0.4 KB in the test, a few KB on the real universe**. `/api/m/screens` returns every screen with its live count, `/api/m/stock` one stock's grouped fields plus its stored headlines.
- **That is only possible because the filter grammar left the page.** `private/filters.js` (commit 9389322) holds `filterValue` / `compileFilter` / `screenRows` — extracted from index.html so the server can evaluate a screen exactly as the browser does. `rowcard.js` became Node-loadable at the same time (`})(typeof window !== 'undefined' ? window : globalThis)`), so `mobileRow()` formats through `RowCard.fieldValues()` — **the same text the hover card and the tiles show**, which is the point: a number cannot read differently on a phone.
- **`readScreens()` returns `group`, not `grp`.** The first cut of `/api/m/screens` read `sc.grp` and every heading on the phone came back `undefined` — caught by the test, invisible to a syntax check.
- **The setup**: up to **6 views of at most 5 fields** each, in `app_meta` under `mobile_config`, cached 60s per instance. `cleanMobileConfig()` rebuilds from scratch against `RowCard.fieldCatalogue()` — unknown field keys dropped, nameless or empty views refused, ids slugified and deduped — the prefs-PUT rule. **An empty list falls back to the three built-in views** (Move / Verdict / Value), so deleting the last one cannot leave a phone with nothing to show.
- **The chosen view lives in `localStorage` (`m.view`)**, which is right for it: it is a per-device convenience, not an account preference, and a guest has no prefs key at all.
- **Charts, and the toggle beside View (2026-09-16, owner's request — "it looks nicer").** On by default, remembered per device (`m.chart`). **The toggle controls the PAYLOAD as much as the display**: `chart=1` is what makes the server draw anything, so with charts off the rows fall back to the compact line they were and nothing is sent. The stock sheet's chart follows the same switch.
- **Guests are filtered server-side on every route**, the CDN incident's lesson: `mobileRows()` narrows to `GUEST_SYMBOLS` before any screen is evaluated, so the counts are over the preview too, and `/api/m/stock` 403s a symbol outside it.
- **Discovery**: a `Phone version` row in the screener's ⋯ menu, and `#phoneBar` — a one-line hint shown on the screener below 720px with a dismissal kept in `localStorage`. Showing or hiding it calls `paintWindow(true)`, since the windowed table sizes itself from its container and no resize event fires (the news ticker's rule).
- Verified: 56 checks against the real server on an in-memory database — the API and its validation, the phone at 390×844 (screens list with counts, a screen's rows, the stock sheet with its news, the view switcher surviving a reload, the three tabs, no sideways scroll, every tap target ≥36px), the charts (absent unless asked for, the thinning, the compressed cost, the line's colour and the area's, the toggle off/on and surviving a reload, the sheet's taller one), the desktop editor saving a rename and a field, the phone picking it up, and the screener's own table still aligned; plus 21 role checks with `ADMIN_PASSWORD` set — anon 302/401, guest limited to its two preview symbols and charted only for those, 403 on the setup, member 302 on the setup page and 403 on its PUT, owner 200 on both.

## Saved posts — the studio on a phone
**A studio card, named and kept, opened on a phone and saved to the camera roll (2026-09-16, owner's idea).** The workflow it exists for: set a card up on the desktop once, then on any day open it on a phone and post it to Instagram without touching a computer.

- **A post stores the CONTROLS, not a picture** — `{id, name, tpl, size, opts}`, the studio's control values by control id. Opening it tomorrow draws tomorrow's data, which is the whole point.
- **Site-wide and admin-curated** (owner's call), in `app_meta` under `promo_presets`, the Tile setup and Mobile setup pattern — these are the brand's posts, not a per-account scratchpad. `GET /api/promo-posts` is **member-only**, `PUT` admin. `cleanPosts()` rebuilds from scratch: template checked against `Cards.ids`, artboard against the three real sizes, up to `POSTS_MAX` 12. **Control ids are shape-checked, not listed** — the studio owns that list and restating it here would drift the first time a control was added; the card builder ignores an id it does not know, exactly as `cleanViews` leaves the column list to the screener.
- **NOT `/api/posts` — the blog owns that path**, registered higher up, and Express takes the first match: the first cut answered the blog's empty list and the studio showed "None yet" with no error anywhere. Renamed to `/api/promo-posts`. Nothing but running it finds this.
- **The phone lists posts by NAME and builds one only when it is opened** (owner's call: "I need to see the Preset name and when it click on it it should open the visualization"). `GET /api/m/posts` is the names — **319 bytes for three** — and `GET /api/m/post?id=` builds that one card from the snapshot as it stands, with `Cards.STYLE`. So the tab costs nothing, the archive read a chart card needs happens on the tap, and **opening a post is what makes it current**. A thumbnail shelf was built first and thrown away.
- **The phone would otherwise need the 1.3MB snapshot and the card module to draw its own** — the same reason `/api/m/screen` formats rows here. `cards.js` got the Node-loadable tail for it (`global.Cards`, and only `injectStyle()` ever touches the DOM).
- **Nothing is cached on the phone but three display choices** (`m.view`, `m.chart`, `m.range`), and there is no service worker — so a reload always hits the network and always brings the latest snapshot. That is the owner's stated rule for this page: **no poller, no restore-on-return; a refresh is how you get new data, and it must actually get it.** Pinned by a test that rewrites the snapshot and re-opens a post.
- **`basketPayload()` was lifted out of `/api/basket`** so the posts endpoint runs the same curve rules rather than a second copy. The studio always reads `name=All` and lets the card narrow it, so one basket per window covers every scope.
- **The video export is gone (2026-09-16, owner's call: "remove the export video option everywhere").** It rendered the animation frame by frame into a MediaRecorder; the Motion controls stay as a preview, and a screen recorder is how that becomes a file now. `Cards.freezeAt()` went with it — it existed only to photograph one instant of the animation — while `motion`, `unfreeze` and the number-counting helpers it shared stay in use.
- **Each saved post carries Replace and Delete as labelled buttons.** The delete was a bare `×` beside the word Replace and read as punctuation rather than as the other choice.
- **Clicking a post reloads the whole panel, and `render()` alone is not enough.** The template and size chips are painted by `paintControls()`; without it the card redrew while the chips stayed lit on whatever was chosen before, which reads as nothing having happened. `openPost` also sets every `*Sector` control first, calls `syncIndustries(true)`, then sets the rest — an industry only exists as an option once its sector is set, and depending on the order the keys happen to be stored in is not a plan. Pinned by a test that drives the real UI, saves a filtered card, moves template / shape / every control away, clicks the post and diffs all 43 controls plus both chips plus the card's own markup — and it was checked against the broken version first, where the two chip assertions fail.
- **`private/cardshot.js` is the rasteriser, and there were THREE copies of it** — promo.html, cards.html, and the phone would have been the third. Extracted: computed styles inlined onto a clone (a serialized SVG sees none of the page's stylesheets), the Google fonts fetched once and embedded as data URIs (an external ref inside an SVG-as-image is dropped, and a face still pointing at the network *taints the canvas*), then foreignObject → canvas at the card's true pixel size. Verified straight after the move: a real 1080×1350 PNG, 538 KB in 249ms, and the studio's own Download button still downloading.
- **The artboard's own styles moved into `cards.js` as `.s-art`**, for the same reason — promo.html and cards.html each had a copy, and the phone would have made three. Each host still owns what is genuinely its own: the studio scales it to the window, `/cards` fixes one shape and adds a drop shadow.
- **The phone's fourth tab, Posts** (hidden from guests: the studio is member-only, and these carry portfolio names and whole-universe rankings the preview withholds). Thumbnails are **the real card scaled**, not a reflowed copy — a 1080px artboard with `transform: scale(measured width / 1080)`, so the picture tapped is the picture saved; the scale is measured after layout rather than computed, so rotating the phone re-fits it. **The frame must be `display:block`** — it is a `<span>` inside a `<button>`, and its height was silently ignored until it was.
- **Tapping a name opens the card itself**, scaled to the sheet, with the data's timestamp on it; **Save as an image** is the second step, because rasterising 1080×1920 on a phone takes a moment and is only wanted when the file is. A screenshot would be the phone's aspect ratio with a status bar in it; the PNG is the exact artboard. The card is built off-screen at 1080 (`#shot`), shot, and removed; the object URL is released on `sheetclose`.
- **Two bugs a screenshot caught that passing assertions did not.** The PNG came out **perfectly sized and completely blank**: `inlineStyles` copies the original's own placement onto the clone, so the off-screen `left:-20000px` went with it and the card rendered outside the canvas — `shoot()` now neutralises the clone's position (to `relative`, not `static`: the card is the containing block for its own aura). And `.sheet h2` beat `.s-title` on specificity, quietly resetting the card's 66px headline to 18px — every sheet rule that a card could also match is a **child** selector now. The tests check ink and computed type, not just dimensions.
- Verified: 29 checks — validation (unknown template, unknown artboard, nameless, junk control ids including `__proto__`, the cap), every post built server-side with its artboard and the chart card actually reading the archive, the studio listing / opening / saving (template, size and controls all restored), the phone's tab and thumbnails (real card, scaled, right shape, no sideways scroll), a 1080×1350 and a 1080×1920 PNG rendered on the phone as blobs, the off-screen stage cleaned up; plus the role walk (guest 403 on both post routes, member read-only, owner writes and every member sees it) and the studio export re-verified after the extraction.

## The admin console
**`/admin` (admin only, the one `Admin` link in the screener's bar) — everything that is not a description of the table (2026-09-14).** Six sections behind a jump bar: **Data** (both refreshes, Database, News runs, Refresh runs, Fill missing, add a ticker, forward returns), **Research** (Cards, Analysis, Strategy, Single, plus the three per-stock pages behind a symbol picker), **Scoring** (the momentum weighting), **Studio** (Promo, member Cards), **People & logs** (Users, Visitors, Activity), **Reference** (Help, Ask, Contact). The bar it replaced carried six pickers and eighteen buttons.

- **The console's refresh rows open `/refreshes` (2026-09-15)** — `/refreshes?do=refresh|refreshall|refreshmissing` — and that tab runs the refresh while its live card, rounds and history update beside it. **The round loop moved into `private/refresh-loop.js` (`RefreshLoop.run(mode, api, hooks)`, `RefreshLoop.runPlain(api)`)**, shared by the screener and `/refreshes`, so there is still exactly one copy of the pacing (62s), the budget (`ceil(work/7)+6`, Fill missing sized from its targets), the refusal allowance (8), the stagnation stop (3) and the closing DELETE; pages pass their own `api()` and draw progress from hooks. `/refreshes` strips `?do=` before acting, refuses to start while a run is already live, warns before the tab is closed mid-run, and says how the run ended (done / nothing to do / stopped / ended short). The screener still honours `?do=` for old links. Verified: 13 loop unit checks (every outcome, run-named rounds, DELETE always sent, budgets), headless runs of all three console actions plus the already-running guard on `/refreshes`, and the screener's Refresh all through the shared loop.
- **The console is a door, not a second machine.** *(Superseded for the refreshes by the entry above.)* Both refreshes are deep links — `/?do=refresh` and `/?do=refreshall` — because the round loop, its budget, the progress banner and the table being refreshed all live on the screener; a second copy of that loop here is exactly the drift `screens.js`, `action.js` and `cards.js` exist to prevent. The forward-returns view is the same: `/admin` picks the date and links to `/?asOf=YYYY-MM-DD`, and the screener still owns the mode, its banner and its column group. **The screener strips the instruction out of the address bar with `history.replaceState` before acting** — a reload that re-ran `do=refreshall` is ten minutes and several hundred API credits.
- **Add a ticker is implemented here**, because it is one API call with no table state behind it, and it reports the resolved company name — a name coming back empty is still the earliest hint of a typo.
- **The weighting EDITOR is here and nowhere else.** It left the screener's bar rather than being copied, so "edited in one place only" still holds; the screener and `/analysis` read the saved set and neither edits it. **The console must hand back every pref it does not touch** — a prefs PUT replaces the whole row, so it merges into what `GET /api/prefs` returned rather than sending the weighting alone. A screener tab left open still holds the old weighting and would write it back on its next pref change, so the save message says to reload it.
- **What the screener kept instead of the picker: `#lensChip`**, shown only while a non-default weighting is in force. The two score columns are tinted whenever the lens is on, and with the picker gone nothing else would say why.
- **"Disable the button" became a flag.** `setPulling()` toggles `body.pulling` (a progress cursor) and guards `refreshAll()` from starting twice; the buttons it used to grey out are on another page.
- **Members are not locked out of anything they had.** `/admin` is admin-only, so the research menu stays in their bar; what they lose is editing the weighting, which `/analysis` still lets them switch by preset.
- Verified on introduction by a route walk (admin 200 on `/admin`, anon redirect, `/admin.html` funnels to `/admin`) and by a check that every `$('id')` the page asks for exists in its own markup — a syntax check cannot see a `TypeError` from a control that was deleted out from under its listener.

## The architecture diagram
**`/architecture` (admin only, an `Architecture` row in the console's Reference section) — every moving part and what talks to what (2026-09-17).** Six bands top to bottom: who is asking, the Vercel edge, the function, persistence, what is scheduled, and what only runs on the owner's machine.

- **The drawing is DATA, not markup.** `BANDS`, `NODES` and `EDGES` are three arrays and a fifty-line renderer emits one SVG; moving a band or renaming a store is an edit to a list rather than to a pile of coordinates. No library, the same call the charts made.
- **SVG text is safe here and nowhere else in this app.** The diagram keeps its own aspect ratio, so glyphs are not distorted — unlike every price chart, which stretches with `preserveAspectRatio="none"` and has to carry HTML labels.
- **Three routing cases, and the middle one was wrong first.** Two boxes in the SAME band have to be joined across, not up and over; the first cut sent `db.js → sessions` climbing out of the band and back down through everything between. And where another box sits between them the line goes UNDER the row, or four stores hanging off one accessor read as a chain passing work along.
- **Edge labels hug the source box on a plate.** Anchored mid-run they landed on a box title whenever an edge skipped a band; a same-band label with less clear room than it needs is dropped rather than printed across two boxes.
- **It restates facts that live in code**, which is the `/help` bargain: the 610-credit ceiling, rows-read metering, nothing running after a response, `public/` being unprotectable. If those change, this page changes with them.
- **Download PNG** goes through the shared `cardshot.js`, so the diagram can be pasted into a document at 1600px.
- Verified: a route walk (anon and guest redirected, `/architecture.html` funnelled, owner 200), and the drawing itself — 23 boxes each with a title, 22 edges all real paths, nothing off the canvas, **no two boxes overlapping**, no sideways scroll at 1500px or 390px, the console's link and its icon resolving, and the PNG coming back with ink in it.

## The data quality page
**`/quality` (admin, a row in the console's Data section) — one line per stock saying what is actually held for it, and what to run about it (2026-09-18).** Proposed and declined once as a fundamentals-coverage page, then built when the evidence changed: **bar depth never converges.** A new ticker gets ~300 bars from its first price pull and stays there until someone runs `backfill-bars`, where `fundamentals_history` days even out on their own. Every gap this app has had was silent — exchange and currency null for the whole universe for weeks, two stocks with no earnings history, one with 66 sessions — and each was found by querying the database by hand.

- **The archive is measured by SPAN, not counted.** `select symbol, count(*) from bars group by symbol` reads 1.08M rows, the shape that produced the Turso quota warning, and no index can answer "how many". `barsSpan()` seeks the first and last bar per symbol instead: **708 rows for 354 stocks against 1.08M**. Sessions are estimated from the span (×0.69) and the page says so in as many words.
- **The small tables ARE counted**, because they are bounded: `fundamentals_history` is universe × recorded days (~2,350 rows), `earnings_history` universe × 40 quarters (~10,400). Those three rollups are allowlisted in `query-plan-test.js` with that reasoning written beside them.
- **It reports the fix, not just the diagnosis** — a ready `backfill-bars.js --commit --depth 1300 --only …` for the thin ones, with a copy button. **`--only` is the point**: each symbol is replaced wholesale, so a blanket depth would truncate the stocks that already hold twenty years. A test asserts the deep stock is never in that list.
- **Nine flags, worst first**, each naming a specific consequence: no bars / too short to score (under `MIN_BARS`, 274 — no momentum score at all) / no 5Y (under 1,260) / stale prices / no profile / profile pull failed (the `fetched_at = 0` sentinel) / no fundamentals / no earnings / no exchange. Every summary card is a filter.
- **`readSnapshot()` was in the first cut and took it from 2.4s to 10.8s.** A ~1.3MB JSON blob, parsed to obtain a display name that `readNamesFull()` answers from two columns. **The seeks were never the cost**; measured 2,366ms for 354 stocks, cached five minutes, `?fresh=1` to force.
- **First real reading, 2026-09-18** (354 stocks): 253 clean, **9 too short to score**, 92 without five years, 1 with stale prices, 2 with no earnings. AVB had **11 sessions and no bar since 2026-08-06** — it had been quietly carrying a month-old close on the screener.
- **Two summary charts above the table (2026-09-20, owner's request).** The page was all counts and rows, and the SHAPE of the archive had to be computed by hand three times in two days. Both are drawn from rows the page has **already fetched** — `firstBar`, `lastBar` and the per-dataset fields — so they cost no query, add no endpoint, and the five-minute cache still holds.
  - **What the archive can price, by year.** One bar per year, counting the stocks whose bars **SPAN** it — not the ones that merely started before it. A stock that stopped trading in 2012 cannot price 2020, and counting it would overstate what a backtest from that year actually had. This is the number that decided everything in the trend backtest and was previously only obtainable by running a script. A year holding under a tenth of the universe is greyed, because it cannot form a basket; the caption names the usable floor and says that **a wall rather than a slope is the 5,000-bar API ceiling, not a listing date**.
  - **What is held, per dataset.** Seven tracks — Bars, 5Y, Profile, Fundamentals, Earnings, Exchange, Headlines — each a share of the universe, coloured green/amber/red, and **clickable to filter the table** through the cards' own `only` mechanism rather than a second one. It turns nine scattered flag counts into one picture, and makes the weakest dataset obvious at a glance (headlines, by a distance).
  - **`display: block` on both the track and the fill.** They are `<span>`s, and an inline box ignores width and height — the first cut set a percentage width on an inline element and drew **nothing**. The track still showed, because a background paints an inline box, so it read as an empty bar rather than as broken markup. **The test passed**: it read the style ATTRIBUTE, which was correct, instead of the rendered box. A screenshot found it, and the assertion now checks `getBoundingClientRect()`.
  - Verified: 24 checks against a fixture where every stock has a different shape — two stocks that DIE in 2012, one alone in 2003-05 so those years are thin, and one recent listing. 2010 must count 5 and 2020 must count 4; the obvious wrong implementation ("first bar before the year") says 6 for 2020 and makes the two years identical.
- **The `.card` class sits on the bezel AND the core** (the double-bezel pattern), so `querySelectorAll('.card')` attached two listeners per card and one click toggled the filter twice. `:scope > .card`. The `.warn` lesson in a new place: a class used for two purposes will be selected for one of them by accident.
- Verified: 32 checks against the real server on an in-memory database — a fixture where every stock has a DIFFERENT defect (deep and clean, a year, four months, none at all, deep but forty days stale), the span and its estimate, each flag told apart, the fix command excluding the deep stock, the cache returning the whole body rather than an empty shell, `fresh=1`, and the page's cards, filtering, note and lack of sideways scroll.

## The database page
**`/database` (admin only, a `Database` row in the console's Data section) lists every table with its exact row and column count (2026-09-15).** `tableStats()` in db.js reads `sqlite_master`, then one **read batch** of `count(*) from pragma_table_info(?)` plus `count(*)` per table — measured 41-152ms for 21 tables and ~397k rows. Views would be listed with columns but not counted (counting a view runs its query); there are none today.

- **`GET /api/db-stats` caches the result five minutes per instance**, because every count reads every row and Turso meters rows read (~397k a count, nearly all `bars`); `?fresh=1` (the page's **Count again**) always counts.
- **Descriptions and areas are a static map in the page** (`TABLES`). A table added later still shows, under *Other*, with no description — add it to the map.
- **`/api/db-stats` must sit below `requireAdmin`'s definition.** The first cut registered it beside the page route near the top of server.js and the server failed to boot with a TDZ ReferenceError — `node --check` passes that; only the route walk caught it.
- Verified: local walk (anon/guest 302 and 403, admin 200, `/database.html` funnels), cached vs fresh responses, counts equal to an independent query.

## The NASDAQ reference list
**`/nasdaq` (admin only, linked from the console's Reference section) — every US listing NASDAQ publishes, in its own table, joined to NOTHING (2026-09-14).** It exists so a candidate ticker can be *found*; adding one to the screener is still a deliberate act, and the owner's instruction was explicit: do not mix this with the screener.

- **Why it exists at all.** Twelve Data will not sell this on the Pro plan at a sane price, measured rather than assumed: `/stocks` is free and lists 16,399 US common stocks but carries **no market cap**; `/profile` (10 credits) has sector, industry and employees but **no cap field either**; `/statistics` (50 credits) is the only Pro source of `market_capitalization` and **does not batch** — `symbol=AAPL,MSFT` returns 404 and still charges 50. Sweeping NYSE + NASDAQ (5,747 names) would be **~287,000 credits and about eight hours** at the 610/minute ceiling. There IS a `/market_cap` endpoint at **1 credit**, and it is **Ultra-only** — it answered for AAPL and 403'd for MSFT and NVDA, the free-sample-symbol trap this file already warns about, caught a second time.
- **The source**: `api.nasdaq.com/api/screener/stocks?tableonly=true&limit=25000&download=true&exchange=<EX>`, free, keyless. All 12 published fields are stored plus the exchange, which the API does not return and the *request* therefore supplies — three calls, NASDAQ 4,131 + NYSE 2,710 + AMEX 295 = **7,136 rows**, of which **4,344 clear $100M** and 398 carry no cap at all.
- **The User-Agent is load-bearing and the failure mode is nasty.** Given a bot-shaped agent the endpoint does not answer 403 — **it never replies at all**. Measured: an honest `TickrLab/1.0` agent hung until a 20s abort; a browser-shaped one answered 200 in 296ms. So `NASDAQ_TIMEOUT_MS` is not belt-and-braces, it is what stops a serverless function sitting on its hands until the platform kills it.
- **One exchange per request**, `POST /api/nasdaq/refresh {exchange}`, driven three times by the page — the Refresh-all shape. Measured server-side: AMEX 0.3s, NYSE 1.0s, NASDAQ 2.7s, so the split is comfortable rather than necessary. Each exchange is a wholesale replace, which is how a delisting leaves.
- **`{ ...row }` on a libSQL Row gives POSITIONAL keys.** The first read shipped `{0: 'AAPL', 1: 'NASDAQ', …}` and every field on the page read `undefined`; named access works, spreading does not. Accessors here build the object field by field.
- **`url` is stored but never shipped** — all 7,136 are exactly `/market-activity/stocks/<lowercase symbol>`, checked, so the page derives it and the payload drops 277 KB. What is left is ~2.0 MB, ~370 KB gzipped, fetched once for an admin page that then filters in the browser.
- **The page marks rows already in the screener** (`held`, from the universe in `/api/portfolios`, NASDAQ's `BRK/B` matched as `BRK.B`) and can filter them out, which is the actual workflow — hunting for what you do not have. **Only symbols flow the other way, and only when ticked** (see below); no NASDAQ value — cap, sector, price — is ever written into the screener.
- **Paste a batch on `/admin` instead (2026-09-18).** The `/nasdaq` table is for FINDING a ticker; when you already have a list, hunting each one through 7,136 rows is the wrong shape. `Add many at once` takes commas, spaces, newlines, `$TSLA`, `brk/b` and lowercase, deduplicates, and **puts the whole batch in one portfolio — creating it if it does not exist**, which the bulk API refused before (`Portfolio not found`).
  - **Creating takes an explicit `createPortfolio: true`**, and the page confirms out loud first. A typo in a batch would otherwise leave a junk list behind with every stock quietly inside it. The name matches **case-insensitively**, like `POST /api/portfolios`: typing `chips` when `Chips` exists lands in the existing one rather than making a second.
  - **Free text splits into symbol-shaped tokens, and that is the real hazard.** `not a ticker!` becomes NOT, A and TICKER — and **A is Agilent**, so neither length nor shape can tell a batch from a sentence. `POST /api/universe/check` seeks the pasted symbols against the stored NASDAQ listing (`symbol` is the primary key, so N seeks, never a scan) and the preview says *2 not in the NASDAQ listing (NOT, TICKER)* in red. **Advisory, never blocking**: a legitimate foreign listing may simply not be in NASDAQ's file.
  - **No API call at add time**, the owner's instruction and the existing behaviour: names come from the stored listing, so adding is free and instant and the data arrives with Fill missing. The preview quotes the cost (~81 credits a stock, 7 a minute) before you commit.
  - **`input[type="text"]` was uppercasing the portfolio name.** The rule was written for the ticker box; it is `#sym` now. The third selector this day written for one element and caught by a later one, after `.warn` and `.card` — **a style rule that is not scoped is a trap with a delay on it.**
- **Bulk add (2026-09-15, owner's choice of the bulk-import options).** A checkbox column (held rows and `^` preferred shares disabled), a header box that selects every MATCHING row in sort order (not just the drawn page), and a selection bar: count, the data-pull cost (~81 credits a stock, 7 a minute of Fill missing), an optional "Also add to <portfolio>", Add. `POST /api/universe/bulk {symbols, portfolio?}` (admin, ≤ `BULK_ADD_MAX` 500): `/` → `.`, `SYMBOL_RE`, dedupe; answers `{added, already, invalid}` plus the universe. **Names come from the listing** (`cleanListingName` strips "Common Stock", "Ordinary Shares", ADR tails), only where none is stored — **so adding costs no credits**; the data arrives with Fill missing (the done bar links `/refreshes?do=refreshmissing`) or the nightly gap fill. **Read the universe BEFORE the portfolio write** — `writePortfolios()` inserts members into the universe, and reading after it reported every new stock as "already there" with no names; caught by the test. A symbol the data provider does not know shows as an error row after the pull and stays a Fill missing "failed" gap until removed. Verified: API caps/404, and the page in headless Chrome against the real server on an in-memory database (23 checks — disabled rows, BRK/B matching, cost line, portfolio add, names trimmed and never overwriting, select-all capped and filter-respecting, held after add, no page errors). Its sector and industry columns are labelled *(NASDAQ)* because they are a third taxonomy agreeing with neither GICS nor the Twelve Data values on the screener.
- The ballast query the research log wants is one filter away: pre-2010 listing, $100M–$2B today — **276 candidates** at the time of writing.

## The promo studio
**The template picker is a dropdown (2026-09-17, owner's call).** Nine templates as a stack of buttons cost most of the panel's height, and the panel is the thing you scroll while tuning a card — the picker is 52px now against roughly 450. The note that used to sit on every button follows the chosen template underneath it, so nothing was lost, and the control matches every other one on the panel. The options are rebuilt only when the LIST changes, since `/api/me` decides whether the owner-only Announcement template is in it and that answer lands after the first paint.

**Every scope in the studio has an Industry picker under its Sector picker (2026-09-15)** — `movIndustry`, `chtIndustry`, `spkIndustry`, `rngIndustry`, `advIndustry`, `fundIndustry`. `scopeOf()` in cards.js derives the industry key from the sector key (`xxSector` → `xxIndustry`), so a host without the control (the member `/cards` page) filters nothing and is unchanged; the kicker names the narrowest cut (the industry alone, since it implies its sector). `syncIndustries()` runs at the top of every studio render and refills an industry list only when its sector changed, offering just that sector's industries (`Cards.industries(rows, sector)`) and dropping a choice the new sector does not hold. Verified headlessly on the live snapshot: 19 checks (placement under Sector in all six, nesting, card rows only semiconductors, reset on sector change, a second template, a host with no control). **Open to every signed-in member since 2026-09-15, from a `Promo Studio` button in the screener's bar after Rules** (hidden for guests, whose `/api/basket` 403s; the route redirects them, and signed-out visitors go to `/login`). **The Announcement template stays the owner's** — `OWNER_ONLY_TPLS` in promo.html filters it from the template list unless `/api/me` says admin; it is free text with no data behind it, so there is nothing server-side to guard. The page now loads `wmark.js` like every member data page (the owner is skipped). The Promo row left the admin console's Studio section, which keeps the Member cards row. Verified: headless template lists per role (member 8 templates without Announcement, owner 9) and a route walk (anon → login, guest → `/`, owner 200). *Originally:* **`/promo` (admin only, ⋯ menu): fixed marketing-card templates rendered from tonight's data in the site's own theme, for manual screenshotting** — no social integration by design (2026-09-14). A real 1080px artboard (4:5 / square / 9:16) scaled to fit the window, so crops line up post after post; every card carries the same chrome (wordmark, date, `tickrlab.com · N stocks screened nightly · Mechanical readings — not investment advice`). Five templates, all client-side from `/api/stocks` (tickers as typographic monogram tiles — logos were removed): **Movers of the week** (top |1W| moves), **The rules changed their mind** (advice changes with the fired rule named — the differentiator card), **Breakout radar** (fresh 3M highs, amber ≥1.5× volume badge), **Where the market stands** (universe by trend state), and **Announcement** (kicker/headline/body free text in the house type). **The no-verdict rule extends to marketing**: cards report what the screen and the rules did, never "stocks to buy" — the owner signed off on that line explicitly. Empty states are written to still be postable ("every verdict held today"). Templates were VM-smoke-tested (four builders + two empty states) and the route walk verified (admin 200, anon/guest redirect, `/promo.html` funnels to `/promo`).

### The 2026-09-19 nightly: a failed workflow over a successful refresh
**The job reported failure; the data was perfect.** The run record read `complete` — 9 rounds, 430 of 430 loaded, 0 refused, 5,001 credits — while GitHub mailed "All jobs have failed" after 26m32s. The same SHAPE as the 2026-09-18 news incident, and again the owner reasonably read it as "the refresh failed" when it had not.

**Two causes, both mine, both from the tech-history work.**

- **A full scan in the refresh tail.** `noteTechMark` asked `techHistorySpan()` for the newest mark — `count(*), count(distinct symbol), min(d), max(d)`, which SCANS. Measured against production at **14 seconds cold, 303ms warm, over 228k rows**, and it ran on EVERY round of a Refresh all. Nine rounds of that is ~2 minutes of waste and ~2M rows against the meter; on the final round it sits in the tail that must finish before the response, so the round was recorded, the run closed — and then time ran out before the report could be built or the job answered. **The stored report is empty and `reportSent` is null, which is how to spot this from the outside.** It is one indexed seek now (`select max(d)`, a covering-index lookup, 0ms), and a test pins the plan.
- **A finished run kept being asked to work.** The job only exits 0 when a round answers `done`. That one response was lost, and every later call fell through to a fresh full refresh, timed out at the job's 180-second curl limit, and counted as a round with no progress — three of those and it gives up and reports failure. **The extra ~8 minutes in the workflow's 26.5 against the run's 24.5 is exactly three of those calls.** The route now answers `{done:true}` at once for a run already `complete` or `incomplete`, the way it already did for `stopped`: measured at **43ms against a 180-second timeout**.

**The lesson, which the news incident already taught and this repeated: work added to the refresh tail is not free, and a slow query there does not fail loudly — it fails as a job that says the night went wrong when it went right.** Before adding anything to that path, time it COLD against production, and prefer a seek. And when a refresh "fails", read the run record before believing it: `complete` with a full `loaded` and an empty report means the work succeeded and the tail did not.

### The heavy reads stand aside while a refresh runs
**Nothing may compete with a refresh for the database (2026-09-21, owner's instruction).** A refresh round reads ~118,671 rows and **cannot wait**: nothing may run after a response on this platform, so the tail has to finish inside the request, and the nightly job gives up after three rounds without progress. A page can wait; a night cannot. The 2026-09-19 nightly is the proof — it reported failure over data that was perfect.

`standAside(res)` in server.js answers **503** with the run's name and its progress, and the four heaviest reads call it:

| route | what it reads |
|---|---|
| `/api/db-stats` (`/database`) | every row of every table — **1.7M**, nearly all `bars` |
| `/api/backtest` | ~157,000 bars, plus the snapshot blob, earnings and recorded fundamentals |
| `/api/trend-backtest` | 76,531 marks |
| `/api/data-quality` (`/quality`) | ~33,000 rollup rows + every profile blob |

- **It guards the READ, not the page.** A cached answer touches nothing, so `/api/db-stats` and `/api/data-quality` still serve theirs, and a trend sweep whose marks are already in memory still runs — `tbMarksWarm()` is one definition of "warm" shared by the loader and the guard, so they cannot disagree. **A guard that refuses free work is a regression dressed as safety.** The test proves both halves at once: the same endpoint is served warm and refused cold in the same run.
- **Validation runs first**, so a malformed request still gets the 400 that explains it rather than being told to come back later and then refused again.
- **The refusal has to be a SENTENCE.** All four pages render `j.error` straight into the page, so a bare status reaches a human as "HTTP 503". `/quality` was the one discarding the body (`throw new Error('HTTP ' + r.status)`) and now reads it.
- **What it covers, and the gap is deliberate.** `readRefreshState()` is non-null only while a **multi-round** run is live — Refresh all, Fill missing, Fast refresh, the nightly. A plain price Refresh writes no state row *on purpose* (a row there raises the refreshing banner for every viewer, thirteen times a trading day), so the ~30-second intraday rounds are not covered. They are not the hazard; the 25-minute run is.
- **No override, and none is needed**: the flag ages out after `REFRESH_STALE_MS`, so a run that dies mid-flight cannot lock the pages out. The answer is cached 5s so a page firing several guarded calls pays one round trip, and **a failure to read the flag counts as not-busy** — a database hiccup must not be able to lock out the research pages.
- **The product is not collateral damage.** `/api/stocks`, `/api/sparklines`, `/api/status` and `/api/basket` are untouched: refusing a member's own page is a regression, not a safeguard.
- Verified: 25 checks against the real server on an in-memory database — all four refused mid-run with the run named and its progress in the text, Retry-After set, cached and warm answers still served, the cold/warm pair on one endpoint, a 400 still a 400, the screener and its polls unaffected, and a stale flag unlocking everything on its own.

### The slowest thing in the app was a row count in the refresh tail
**`archiveStats()` ran `select count(*), max(d) from bars` — over 1,708,408 rows, uncached — in the tail that must finish before the response, on every report build (2026-09-20).** Measured cold against production:

| | |
|---|---|
| `count(*) from bars` | **135.9s** |
| `max(d) from bars` | **268.9s** |
| `d order by d desc limit 1` | 253.0s |
| `max(d)` again, same connection | 0.9s |
| `readSnapshot()` beside it | 2.1s |

- **How it showed.** A plain one-round price refresh, the simplest operation here, answered **HTTP 504 after 300.1s** — Vercel's gateway ceiling. The round itself was 99.6s and the snapshot was written correctly at +100s; the remaining ~200s was this. So the work succeeded, the response died, the run was swept `abandoned`, and the night reads as a failure. **That is the third time this shape has bitten** — news (2026-09-18), tech-history (2026-09-19), this — and each time the tail was the cause.
- **`max(d)` is the trap, not the count.** It looks like the cheap half and is twice as expensive, because there is **no index on `bars(d)`** — the primary key is `(symbol, d)`. Its plan reads `SEARCH bars USING COVERING INDEX`, the second time in one day a SEARCH line concealed a minutes-long walk (see `select distinct d` in the trend backtest). **"Drop the count, keep the through-date" would have been a 2x regression shipped as a fix**, and it was what I proposed before measuring.
- **Neither is needed.** The report's `Prices as of` already carries that date from `asOf`, computed from rows in memory; the **Bar archive line is deleted** rather than made cheap, since it said the same thing twice. `/api/refresh-runs/health` wanted only the through-date and **threw the row count away** — it uses `barsThrough()` now, which is `barsMaxDates()`: one indexed seek per symbol on the primary key. Its own comment had called it "the slower half of the page" and blamed the profiles. The archive's SIZE stays on `/database` and `/quality`, cached, where counting is the point of the page.
- **`sendRefreshReport` took a `snap` argument** so the two refresh-tail callers hand over the payload instead of re-reading the 1.3MB snapshot they just wrote — 2.1s, and the same trap `/quality` records (readSnapshot took that page from 2.4s to 10.8s).
- **`query-plan-test.js` could never have caught this, and that is fixed too.** Its allowlist carried a bare `/^select count\(\*\)/`, justified for `/database`, which silently licensed every count over every big table. It is now **eight named entries** — barsStats, fundamentalsStats, techHistorySpan, the two clear-with-count confirms, the two log summaries, the news coverage tile — each saying where it runs. The file now also states what it cannot do: **it reads plans, not call sites, and a count that is fine on a cached admin page is a disaster in the refresh tail while the SQL looks identical.**
- Verified: the guard fails if the `bars` aggregate returns, and the report suites assert the Bar archive line is gone while `Prices as of` still carries the date. **Against production, the same call that 504'd:**

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| a plain one-round refresh | **504 at 300.1s** | **200 at 146.2s** |
| its run record | `abandoned` | **`complete`** |
| `/api/refresh-runs/health` | ~145s+ | **4.4s cold, 0.7s warm** |

- **AND THE PHASES FINALLY ARRIVED, which corrects the standing claim about what a round costs.** The response carrying `phases` is the thing a 504 destroys, so this was unmeasurable until the tail was fixed:

  `profiles 7.0s · prices 67.1s · score 0.3s · trend-bars 18.4s · persist-bars 20.4s` — 113.2s of phases inside a 146.2s wall, at 430 symbols.

  **The dominant cost of a refresh round is the PROVIDER, not the database**: 67.1s of 113s is the batched `time_series` call. The database's share is ~39s (`trend-bars` + `persist-bars`), and **`trend-bars` — the 650-day window this file names as the thing to watch — is 18.4s**, not the bulk of it. A previous reading of this round as "~100s of largely database time" was wrong in its attribution; the round's own `ms` measures everything, and only the phases say where it goes. Note also that prices are already paced above ~529 symbols, so at 1,000 that phase is capped by the credit budget by design rather than growing without bound.

### The 2026-09-20 nightly: a benchmark run against production during the run
**The failure was mine and it was not in the code — it was a 106.7MB query aimed at the live database at 4:26 PM on a nightly day.** Run 49 went `abandoned` after one round, 370/430 loaded, and GitHub mailed a failure.

| | |
|---|---|
| 16:24:49 | the workflow starts |
| 16:25:02 | the server records run 49 |
| **16:26:09 → 16:28:15** | **my whole-table benchmark: 332,883 rows, 106.7MB, 126.2s** |
| 16:27:56 | the workflow dies — 187s, its `-m 180` curl limit |
| 16:28:43 | the server finishes round 1 (106.5s of compute), 47s too late |

- **The contention runs BOTH ways, and this file only documented one.** The `init()` section records that a long refresh makes every other tab cold. The reverse is just as true and bit harder: **a long query makes the refresh miss its deadline.** The round was 106.5s of compute where a normal one is a fraction of that, the start call overran 180 seconds, and nothing called `DELETE`, so the flag aged out and the run was swept to `abandoned`.
- **A deploy was NOT the cause, though three landed minutes later.** The Actions API settles it: the workflow died at 20:27:56Z and the first push was 16:29:06 EDT, afterwards. Worth checking rather than assuming — `run_started_at` / `updated_at` on `/actions/runs/<id>` is public even when the log is not, and it is the cheapest way to time a workflow's death against anything else.
- **What it actually cost was small, and reading the run record is how you know that**: prices were pulled live for all 430 and the snapshot was written at 16:26:49, so the day's data was in. The one real gap was the profile rotation — 2 of ~62 — which self-heals, since each night expires the oldest `ceil(N/7)` again. The failure email described a night that had mostly succeeded, for the third time (see 2026-09-18 news and 2026-09-19 tech-history).
- **The rule this leaves: research reads belong on the LOCAL copy, and anything whole-table aimed at production must not run between 4:15 and 4:45 PM Eastern.** `analysis-db.js` exists precisely so a 100MB question never has to be asked of the live database; the benchmark above had a reason to hit production (it was measuring production's own wall) and still should have waited for the window to clear.

## The trend-only backtest (built and measured)
**A backtest that reads NOTHING but bars, so it can go back twenty years instead of two months (2026-09-19, owner's request).** The advice backtest is capped at two months because `fundamentals_history` begins 2026-08-30 and everything before that would be imputed. Trend, entry, RSI, volume, the moving averages and the 52-week position are all bar-derived, and `action.js` already carries a fundamentals-free rule list — **`etfRules`**, reached whenever a row has neither Quality nor a forward P/E. So there is no new engine and no fork: this runs the same rules the ETF rows run today, which is also what `action-backtest.js` has replayed over eighteen years.
